Preserve the separation between deterministic CRM state and AI-assisted development/orchestration.\n6\t\n7\t## Required workflow\n8\t\n9\t1. Read `PRODUCT.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` and the relevant module files before changing code. Before product, positioning, roadmap or public-distribution decisions, read `docs/strategy/MASTER_PLAN.md` first; for implementation details, `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `DECISIONS.md` remain authoritative.\n10\t2. For a multi-file feature or significant refactor, create and maintain an ExecPlan under `docs/plans/` following `.agent/PLANS.md`.\n11\t3. Use public module services and named workflows. Never mutate CRM tables from API, MCP or UI code.\n12\t4. Any write operation must retain validation, audit and trace.\n13\t5. Keep MCP write tools narrow; destructive or code-generating tools must default to dry-run or require an explicit apply flag.\n14\t6. Do not add a production dependency unless it removes more complexity than it adds; record the reason in `DECISIONS.md`.\n15\t7. Run `npm run verify` before considering work complete.\n16\t8. Update `TASKS.md` and relevant docs when behavior changes.\n17\t9. **Domain-package work requires the `build-custom-domain-package` skill** (`.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`) and `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`. A package imports only `packages/core/index.js`, reaches another package only through a declared capability, and is registered by one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. A single custom object is a module, not a package.\n18\t10. Follow `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` for every feature PR, and **use the `adversarial-review` skill for any milestone review or pre-merge review task** (`.claude/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`). A milestone that skipped the review is unreviewed, not finished.\n19\t11. Respect the core budget rule (ADR-018): **new domain-specific business behavior does not go into `packages/core`** unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability. A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say which runtime capability it is and why a domain package cannot own it.\n20\t12. Read `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` for what is true in the repository today — merged milestone, the commit the public numbers were measured at, open PRs, production blockers — and update it in the same PR as a milestone merge. Do not put volatile status in `MASTER_PLAN.md`. **It carries no test count**: a count is measured into `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` by `node scripts/measure-suite.js --apply` and cited from there (ADR-027). Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n21\t13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n22\t14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n23\t15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n24\t16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n25\t\n26\t## Parallel coding agents\n27\t\n28\tSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\n29\tany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n30\t\n31\t1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n32\t   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n33\t   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n34\t   lands in another agent's tree.\n35\t2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n36\t   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n37\t   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n38\t3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n39\t   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n40\t   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n41\t   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n42\t   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n43\t   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n44\t   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n45\t   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n46\t\n47\tSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n48\t\n49\t## Coding conventions\n50\t\n51\t- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n52\t- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n53\t- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n54\t- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n55\t- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n56\t- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n57\t- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n58\t\n59\t## Definition of done\n60\t\n61\t- behavior works end-to-end;\n62\t- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n63\t- trace and audit are visible;\n64\t- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n65\t- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n66\t- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n67\t\n68\t## The DX Simplicity Gate\n69\t\n70\tBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\n71\tanswer these in the PR. AI agents interpret the user's intent and compose the system, but state changes pass through services and workflows.\n\n## Medusa-inspired mapping\n\n| Medusa concept | Accordo equivalent |\n|---|---|\n| Commerce module | CRM domain module |\n| Product/customer/order | Offer/company/opportunity or contract |\n| Workflow | Commercial process |\n| Provider | Email, calendar, ERP or enrichment adapter |\n| Admin extension | CRM workspace extension |\n| Cloud CLI | Serve, migrate, inspect, trace and deploy commands |\n| Docs MCP | Project schema, workflow and operational context |\n| Agent skills | Repeatable module/workflow implementation instructions |\n\n## Layers\n\n```text\nAdmin / API / SDK\n        ↓\nWorkflows and policies\n        ↓\nCRM module services\n        ↓\nSQLite adapter\n\nMCP + CLI sit beside the application and invoke the same public layer.\nTrace + audit observe every controlled mutation.\n```\n\n## Modules\n\nA module owns one domain boundary. It exposes a service and metadata. Other modules do not update its tables directly.\n\nCurrent modules:\n\n- Company\n- Contact\n- Opportunity\n- Approval\n\n## Workflows\n\nA workflow is a named sequence of traced steps. It coordinates modules and providers. A step can optionally define compensation for reversible side effects.\n\nCurrent workflows:\n\n- `request-opportunity-stage-change`\n- `decide-opportunity-approval`\n\n## Actions\n\nA record action is a lifecycle operation on a single record that is more than a field edit — qualify a lead, close an opportunity. Actions are code-first definitions (`packages/actions/generated/index.js`) exposed through one generic route, SDK method and Admin control rendered from metadata, never per-action generated files and never a declarative DSL.\n\nThe action runtime wraps `execute` in the guarantees a hand-rolled service method would not have: all business writes in one outer transaction (generated-service savepoints nest inside it), domain events held in a transaction-scoped outbox and dispatched only after commit, and a workflow trace written after the transaction resolves so a failed action still leaves a record. Fields a workflow owns are declared `writable: \"managed\"` in the manifest and refused by generic CRUD, so lifecycle state is enforced at the service boundary rather than in the UI.\n\nUse an action for a single-record lifecycle step; use a workflow for a multi-record or multi-step process, or when a human approval gate is involved. See `docs/ACTIONS.md`, ADR-011 and ADR-012.\n\n## Domain packages\n\nDomain behavior lives in optional packages built on the runtime's contracts, not in the core (ADR-018). A package declares itself as plain data — a name, its record manifests, its versioned policies, its actions and function-free schema metadata — and a project enables it with one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. The runtime validates the declaration, fingerprints each policy version (ADR-015), registers the actions and publishes the metadata under `/api/schema`; it never learns the domain's vocabulary. Removing the import removes the domain, and everything else keeps working.\n\n`packages/contracts/` (Milestone 12: contract activation and subscriptions) is the first one, and a third-party package uses the identical path. See `docs/CONTRACT_ACTIVATION.md` and the ADR-018 addendum.\n\n## Providers\n\nProviders isolate external systems from business logic. The proof of concept includes a notification provider contract and an in-memory implementation. Email, calendar, ERP and marketing providers belong here later.\n\n## MCP responsibilities\n\nMCP exposes:\n\n- project and CRM context as resources;\n- controlled application operations as tools;\n- reusable build/debug prompts.\n\nIt does not bypass services or workflows.\n\n## Extension rule\n\nA new business feature should normally add or change only:\n\n1. one module;\n2. one workflow;\n3. one API or MCP surface;\n4. tests;\n5. docs/decision record when architecture changes.\n","numLines":92,"startLine":1,"totalLines":92}}}

Merged in PR #22. It does **not** aggregate JTBD or quality-gate evidence, and does not open any database.\n55\t- [x] **M14b1 — Delivery economics.** A versioned fingerprinted cost policy, append-only time and expense evidence, an immutable versioned economic plan and a reproducible contribution estimate grouped by currency, plus the `delivery-economics@1` capability (`docs/DELIVERY_ECONOMICS.md`). Merged in PR #23 after an adversarial review that fixed three defects: a recurring obligation set against a spend to date, a silently substituted cost-policy version, and a divergent retry absorbed under a reused key. A contribution estimate now exists **only** where every commercial input in that currency is one-time.\n56\t- [x] **AX2 — Machine-readable Solution Plans (platform).** `solutionPlanContract: 1` with a revision and canonical fingerprint, six decision types over the repository's own hierarchy, six evidence categories with resolving citations, a closed approval vocabulary, and `accordo solution inspect|validate|check` — `check` binds a plan to this project's `app inspect` report and reports `PLAN_STALE` (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`, ADR-020). Merged in PR #24 after an adversarial review that fixed seven defects, including an open key set on plan objects, a citation graph with no direction, and a composition binding that read the plan rather than the project. It is **not a planner and not a runtime**: nothing writes a plan, nothing executes one, and a plan may not carry a command.\n57\t- [x] **M14b2 — Delivery change requests, deliverables and acceptance.** Merged in PR #25. Split out of M14b because four models with four different invariants is not one reviewable PR. Scope, intact: governed non-commercial replans, the `pending_commercial_followup` handoff that never touches a signed Order, deliverable evidence, and recorded customer acceptance — which is evidence a local user actor recorded, never an authenticated customer, a legal signature or a billing trigger. Merged (`docs/DELIVERY_CHANGE_ACCEPTANCE.md`): six append-only records, seven human-only actions, two additive capabilities, a package-scoped Admin section (the framework has no seam for a package to ship Admin code — AX1 reports `ADMIN_EXTENSIONS_UNSUPPORTED` — so the section lives in the Admin app and renders only while the package's schema metadata is published), the full starter journey, and an acceptance request that freezes its exact scope with a fingerprint the evidence binds to. Evidence: fault injection on every write, two-connection races, exact reads past 500 rows, exact audit/event/trace counts, AX1 and AX2 integration, upgrade and detach, and 22 real-Chromium checks run as the pre-merge gate. The independent adversarial review added the missing end to the commercial handoff — a raised candidate could otherwise have blocked a project's acceptance evidence permanently — and took the unverified customer label out of the scope fingerprint. The commercial boundary is proved by fingerprinting every commercial row before and after. Acceptance is evidence a user actor recorded — never an authenticated customer, a legal signature or authorization to bill.\n58\t\n59\t- [x] **DX6 — Scenario evidence (platform).** `accordo scenario run <scenario> --json`:\n60\t  a checked-in declarative scenario names a journey by id from a frozen registry in\n61\t  the runner's own source, the runner executes that journey, inspects the\n62\t  application it composed through AX1, answers observations from a closed\n63\t  vocabulary and resolves the scenario's claims against `docs/benchmarks/jobs.json`\n64\t  — publishing both the rows one run established and the counted, sectioned,\n65\t  enumerated set it did **not** (`docs/SCENARIO_EVIDENCE.md`,\n66\t  `docs/plans/dx6-scenario-runner.md`). `scenarioRunContract: 1`; no duration,\n67\t  timestamp, temporary path or machine layout enters the report at all, so two runs\n68\t  are byte-identical. A scenario **cannot carry a command** — no field could hold\n69\t  one, every string is refused if it looks like one, and there is no code path from\n70\t  a document value to an invocation — and a document with any problem starts\n71\t  nothing. It **promotes no JTBD row**: the index is read-only, the claim vocabulary\n72\t  is deliberately not the four-value status vocabulary, and a person still decides.\n73\t  Coverage is claimed rather than discovered, it drives no browser, and it speaks\n74\t  for one composition — all published limitation codes.\n75\t\n76\t- [x] **DX6 — a second business consumer of the scenario contract.**\n77\t  `examples/scenarios/service-sla-escalation.scenario.json` over a second\n78\t  checked-in journey, `examples/journeys/service-sla-escalation/journey.mjs`\n79\t  (`docs/plans/dx6-second-scenario.md`). Deliberately unlike the first: Service is\n80\t  package-native, has a declared state machine and real clock semantics, and the\n81\t  journey composes **contracts + service and nothing else** on an **injected,\n82\t  stepped clock** — so the first-response SLA boundary is observed exactly, `at_risk`\n83\t  at the due instant and `breached` one millisecond later, which no wall-clock run\n84\t  can reach. Serving it changed the contract in three places: journey evidence\n85\t  gained stated **facts** beside numeric counts, the report publishes **which clock**\n86\t  produced the evidence, and limitations gained a **scope** so a journey declares its\n87\t  own. `scenarioRunContract: 2`; the document contract stayed at 1, because every\n88\t  v1 scenario still validates. Four actions ship and these are all of them — `plan-renewal` (reads, writes nothing), `record-renewal-decision`, `request-commercial-followup`, `resolve-commercial-followup` (`packages/lifecycle/README.md`, `docs/plans/m16a-renewal-expansion-operations.md`). **It records intent and hands off.** It renews nothing, cancels nothing, ends nothing, signs nothing, prices nothing, invoices nothing, schedules nothing and recognises no churn; `record-successor` and `record-expansion-intent` were described in an early draft and **never built**, and no successor is linked anywhere. Dry-run by default, `--apply` to write, one atomic `rename` as the commit point, and it refuses rather than guesses: a non-empty target, a target that is not a directory, a target overlapping the framework source, and an invalid npm name — the last with a suggestion it never applies. It reaches **no network**, composes **no domain package**, opens **no database** and imports **no part of the framework**, because it is the one command that must run before the framework exists on disk. **Nothing is published**: `accordo@0.0.1` and `create-accordo@0.0.1` remain empty name reservations, so `npm create accordo` still installs nothing; `packages/create-accordo/package.json` is `private: true`, and `scripts/distribution-check.js` fails if that flag and `site/brand.json`'s registry status ever disagree. Evidence: fault injection on every write, two-connection races, exact reads past 500 rows, exact audit/event/trace counts, AX1 and AX2 integration, upgrade and detach, and 22 real-Chromium checks run as the pre-merge gate. A scenario **cannot carry a command** — no field could hold\n  one, every string is refused if it looks like one, and there is no code path from\n  a document value to an invocation — and a document with any problem starts\n  nothing. Then **Work v1** (PR #70, ADR-030): one package-native Task and Activity domain, held against an opaque subject envelope, consumed by three business events through a declared capability that refuses to write unless it is inside the caller's transaction. It claims no `M`-number, because taking one would assert a position in a sequence a horizontal capability does not have. Its adversarial review found seven defects, one of them a reachable half Task/Activity pair, and answered the browser-only timeline defect with a new shared read surface (ADR-008 addendum 2). Then **DX10** (PR #71, ADR-031): `accordo solution verify <plan> --evidence <doc>` grades each requirement of a checked-in Solution Plan against authorities that ran in the same invocation, over an evidence document that has **no status field** — it may point at proof and never declare it. Its review found eight defects, two critical: an evidence author could reach `verified` by labelling a behavioural requirement `structural`, and a requirement could be verified from a run of a **different application**. The first of those reached exit 0 through the real CLI on this repository before it was closed. |\n| Measured at | `0f501a9` — the commit `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` names. This row repeats the ledger and measures nothing. |\n| Tests | Measured, never typed. `npm run verify` is green on a clean tree at the commit above; **how many** tests that was lives in `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` and in no other file (ADR-027). |\n| Smoke | `npm run smoke` green |\n| Starter | `examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/install.mjs` green from an empty project |\n| Browser smoke | Real-Chromium checks remain manual and are **not in CI** — no workflow launches a browser, and `npm run smoke` is an in-process application smoke (`docs/ADMIN_SMOKE.md`). The Work v1 section has a **30-check** block, all passing, driven twice at `184e543`; it covers that section only and re-runs none of the earlier blocks. A package declares itself as plain data — a name, its record manifests, its versioned policies, its actions and function-free schema metadata — and a project enables it with one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`.
